∎

Subsequently, we have the following theorem.

Theorem 6.3
.

In the root system
\varDelta
of Theorem
6.2
,

\displaystyle\varPi=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\}

is a fundamental root system of
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, where

\alpha_{1}=-\tau_{1}+(1/3)\nu+r,\alpha_{2}=-2r,\alpha_{3}=(-1/2)\tau_{2}-(1/3)\nu+r,\alpha_{4}=(-1/2)\tau_{3}+(2/3)\nu
.
The Dynkin diagram of
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is given by

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

Proof..

The remainders of positive roots are expressed by
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4},
as follows:

\displaystyle(-1/2)(\tau_{2}-\tau_{3})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle(1/2)(\tau_{3}-\tau_{1})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle(1/2)(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{2}+2\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle\tau_{1}+(2/3)\nu

\displaystyle=\alpha_{2}+2\alpha_{3}+2\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle-\tau_{2}-(2/3)\nu

\displaystyle=\alpha_{2}+2\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle\tau_{3}+(2/3)\nu

\displaystyle=2\alpha_{1}+3\alpha_{2}+4\alpha_{3}+2\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle(-1/2)\tau_{1}+(2/3)\nu

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle(-1/2)\tau_{2}+(2/3)\nu

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+2\alpha_{2}+3\alpha_{3}+2\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle-\tau_{1}+(1/3)\nu-r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}

\displaystyle-\tau_{2}+(1/3)\nu-r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+3\alpha_{2}+4\alpha_{3}+2\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle\tau_{3}+(1/3)\nu+r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+2\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle(1/2)\tau_{1}+(1/3)\nu-r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle(1/2)\tau_{3}+(1/3)\nu-r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+2\alpha_{2}+2\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle(1/2)\tau_{1}+(1/3)\nu+r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle(-1/2)\tau_{2}-(1/3)\nu-r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle(1/2)\tau_{3}+(1/3)\nu+r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+2\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle-\tau_{2}+(1/3)\nu+r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+2\alpha_{2}+4\alpha_{3}+2\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle\tau_{3}-(1/3)\nu+r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+2\alpha_{2}+2\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle\nu+r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+2\alpha_{3}+2\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle\nu-r

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+2\alpha_{2}+2\alpha_{3}+2\alpha_{4}.

Hence we see that
\varPi=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\}
is a fundamental root system of
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
. Let the real part
\mathfrak{h}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
of
\mathfrak{h}_{8}
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}=\left\{R_{8}=(\varPhi,0,0,r,0,0)\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\begin{array}[]{l}\varPhi=\varPhi(\phi,0,0,\nu)\in\mathfrak{h}_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}},\\
\quad\phi=\tilde{T}_{0}\in\mathfrak{h}_{6,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}},\\
\qquad T_{0}=\tau_{1}E_{1}+\tau_{2}E_{2}+\tau_{3}E_{3}\in({\mathfrak{J}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}})_{0},\\
\qquad\qquad\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}+\tau_{3}=0,\tau_{i}\in\mbox{\boldmath$R$},\\
\quad\nu\in\mbox{\boldmath$R$},\\
r\in\mbox{\boldmath$R$}\end{array}\right\}.

The Killing form
B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
of
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is given by
B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R_{1},R_{2})=(9/4)B_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(\varPhi_{1},\varPhi_{2})+(9/2)\{Q_{1},P_{2}\}-(9/2)\{P_{1},Q_{2}\}+36r_{1}r_{2}+18t_{1}s_{2}+18s_{1}t_{2},R_{i}=(\varPhi_{i},P_{i},Q_{i},r_{i},s_{i},t_{i}),i=1,2
(Theorem
6.1
), so is on
\mathfrak{h}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
.
Hence, for
R=(\varPhi(\tau_{1}\tilde{E}_{1}+\tau_{2}\tilde{E}_{2}+\tau_{3}\tilde{E}_{3},0,0,\nu),0,0,r,0,0),{R}^{\prime}=({\varPhi}^{\prime}({\tau_{1}}^{\prime}\tilde{E}_{1}+{\tau_{2}}^{\prime}\tilde{E}_{2}+{\tau_{3}}^{\prime}\tilde{E}_{3},0,0,\nu^{\prime}),0,0,r^{\prime}\allowbreak,0,0)\in\mathfrak{h}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
, we have

\displaystyle B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R,{R}^{\prime})=9(\tau_{1}{\tau_{1}}^{\prime}+\tau_{2}{\tau_{2}}^{\prime}+\tau_{3}{\tau_{3}}^{\prime})+12\nu\nu^{\prime}+36rr^{\prime}.

Indeed, it follows from
B_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(\varPhi,{\varPhi}^{\prime})=4(\tau_{1}{\tau_{1}}^{\prime}+\tau_{2}{\tau_{2}}^{\prime}+\tau_{3}{\tau_{3}}^{\prime})+(16/3)\nu\nu^{\prime}
that

\displaystyle B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R,{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}B_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(\varPhi,{\varPhi}^{\prime})+36rr^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}\cdot\left(4(\tau_{1}{\tau_{1}}^{\prime}+\tau_{2}{\tau_{2}}^{\prime}+\tau_{3}{\tau_{3}}^{\prime})+\displaystyle\frac{16}{3}\nu\nu^{\prime}\right)+36rr^{\prime}

\displaystyle=9(\tau_{1}{\tau_{1}}^{\prime}+\tau_{2}{\tau_{2}}^{\prime}+\tau_{3}{\tau_{3}}^{\prime})+12\nu\nu^{\prime}+36rr^{\prime}.

Now, the canonical elements
R_{\alpha_{1}},R_{\alpha_{2}},R_{\alpha_{3}},R_{\alpha_{4}}\in\mathfrak{h}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
corresponding to
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}
are determined as follows:

\displaystyle R_{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle=(\varPhi(\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}(-4\tilde{E}_{1}+2\tilde{E}_{2}+\tilde{E}_{3}),0,0,\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}),0,0,\displaystyle\frac{1}{36},0,0),

\displaystyle R_{\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle=(0,0,0,-\displaystyle\frac{2}{36},0,0),

\displaystyle R_{\alpha_{3}}

\displaystyle=(\varPhi(\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}(\tilde{E}_{1}-2\tilde{E}_{2}+\tilde{E}_{3}),0,0,-\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}),0,0,\displaystyle\frac{1}{36},0,0),

\displaystyle R_{\alpha_{4}}

\displaystyle=(\varPhi(\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}(\tilde{E}_{1}+\tilde{E}_{2}-2\tilde{E}_{3}),0,0,\displaystyle\frac{2}{36}),0,0,0,0,0).

Indeed, let
R=(\varPhi^{\prime},0,0,r^{\prime},0,0)\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
and set
R_{\alpha_{1}}=(\varPhi,0,0,r,0,0)\in\mathfrak{h}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
, then from
B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R_{\alpha_{1}},R)=\alpha_{1}(R)
, we have

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=-\displaystyle\frac{4}{54},\tau_{2}=\tau_{3}=\displaystyle\frac{2}{54},\nu=\displaystyle\frac{1}{36},r=\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}.

Hence we have the required one as
R_{\alpha_{1}}
. For the remainders of
R_{\alpha_{i}},i=2,3,4,5,6
, as in the case above, we obtain the required results.
Note that
R_{\alpha_{1}}
is also given as follows:

\displaystyle R_{\alpha_{1}}=\left(B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}({\underset{\dot{}}{E_{1}}}^{-},{\dot{E_{1}}}_{-})\right)^{-1}[{\underset{\dot{}}{E_{1}}}^{-},{\dot{E_{1}}}_{-}],

where
{\underset{\dot{}}{E_{1}}}^{-}:=(0,\dot{E_{1}},0,0,0,0),{\dot{E_{1}}}_{-}:=(0,0,\underset{\dot{}}{E_{1}},0,0,0)
are the root vectors associated the roots
\alpha_{1},-\alpha_{1}
, respectively. For the remainders of
R_{\alpha_{i}},i=2,3,4
, as in the case above, we obtain the required results.

With above, we see that

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1})

\displaystyle=B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R_{\alpha_{1}},R_{\alpha_{1}})=9\cdot\left(\left(-\displaystyle\frac{4}{54}\right)\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{4}{54}\right)+\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}+\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+12\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}+36\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}=\displaystyle\frac{1}{9},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle=B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R_{\alpha_{1}},R_{\alpha_{2}})=36\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{36}\right)=-\displaystyle\frac{1}{18},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3})

\displaystyle=B_{8}(R_{\alpha_{1}},R_{\alpha_{3}})=9\cdot\left(\left(-\displaystyle\frac{4}{54}\right)\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}+\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\right)+\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+12\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\right)+36\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}=0,

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{4})

\displaystyle=B_{8}(R_{\alpha_{1}},R_{\alpha_{4}})=9\cdot\left(\left(-\displaystyle\frac{4}{54}\right)\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}+\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}+\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+12\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\right)=0,

\displaystyle(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle=B_{8}(R_{\alpha_{2}},R_{\alpha_{2}})=36\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{36}\right)\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{36}\right)=\displaystyle\frac{1}{9},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3})

\displaystyle=B_{8}(R_{\alpha_{2}},R_{\alpha_{3}})=36\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{36}\right)=-\displaystyle\frac{1}{18},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{4})

\displaystyle=B_{8}(R_{\alpha_{2}},R_{\alpha_{4}})=0,

\displaystyle(\alpha_{3},\alpha_{3})

\displaystyle=B_{8}(R_{\alpha_{3}},R_{\alpha_{3}})=9\cdot\left(\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}+\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\right)\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\right)+\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+12\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\right)\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\right)+36\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}=\displaystyle\frac{1}{18},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})

\displaystyle=B_{8}(R_{\alpha_{3}},R_{\alpha_{4}})=9\cdot\left(\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}+\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\right)\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+12\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{1}{36}\right)\cdot\displaystyle\frac{2}{36}=-\displaystyle\frac{1}{36},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{4},\alpha_{4})

\displaystyle=B_{8}(R_{\alpha_{4}},R_{\alpha_{4}})=9\cdot\left(\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{1}{54}+\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\right)\cdot\left(-\displaystyle\frac{2}{54}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+12\cdot\displaystyle\frac{2}{36}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{2}{36}=\displaystyle\frac{1}{18}.

Hence, since we have

\displaystyle\cos\theta_{12}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\frac{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})}{\sqrt{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1})(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2})}}=-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2},\quad\cos\theta_{13}=\displaystyle\frac{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3})}{\sqrt{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1})(\alpha_{3},\alpha_{3})}}=0,

\displaystyle\cos\theta_{14}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\frac{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{4})}{\sqrt{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1})(\alpha_{4},\alpha_{4})}}=0,\quad\cos\theta_{23}=\displaystyle\frac{(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3})}{\sqrt{(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2})(\alpha_{3},\alpha_{3})}}=-\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}},

\displaystyle\cos\theta_{24}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\frac{(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{4})}{\sqrt{(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2})(\alpha_{4},\alpha_{4})}}=0,\quad\cos\theta_{34}=\displaystyle\frac{(\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})}{\sqrt{(\alpha_{3},\alpha_{3})(\alpha_{4},\alpha_{4})}}=-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{3},\alpha_{3})

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\frac{1}{18}<\displaystyle\frac{1}{9}=(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}),

we can draw the Dynkin diagram.
∎

We consider the following complex Lie group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
which is defined by replacing
\mathfrak{C}
with
R
in the complex Lie group
{E_{8}}^{C}
:

\displaystyle(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}

\displaystyle=\mbox{\rm{Aut}}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})

\displaystyle=\left\{\alpha\in\mbox{\rm{Iso}}_{C}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\alpha[R_{1},R_{2}]=[\alpha R_{1},\alpha R_{2}]\right\},

where
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
have been already defined in the beginning of this section.

The immediate aim is to prove the connectedness of the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
. In order to prove it, we will use the manner used in
[
1
]
.

First, we will study the following subgroup
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{\tilde{1},1^{-},1_{-}}
of
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
:

\displaystyle((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{\tilde{1},1^{-},1_{-}}=\left\{\alpha\in(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\alpha\tilde{1}=\tilde{1},\alpha 1^{-}=1^{-},\alpha 1_{-}=1_{-}\right\}.

Then we have the following proposition.

Proposition 6.4
.

The group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{\tilde{1},1^{-},1_{-}}
is isomorphic to the group
(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
:

((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{\tilde{1},1^{-},1_{-}}\cong(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
.

Proof..

This proposition can be proved as in the proof of
[
8
, Proposition 5.7.1]
by replacing
\mathfrak{C}
with
R
.
∎

Subsequently, we will study the following subgroup
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
of
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
:

\displaystyle((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}=\left\{\alpha\in(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\alpha 1_{-}=1_{-}\right\}.

Then we have the following lemma.

Lemma 6.5
.

(1)
The Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
of the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is given by

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}=\left\{R=(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t)\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\begin{array}[]{l}\varPhi\in(\mathfrak{e}_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C},P,Q\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C},r,s,t\in C\end{array}\right\}.

In particular, we have
\dim_{C}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})=21+14\times 2+3=52
.

(2)
The Lie algebra
((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
of the group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
is given by

\displaystyle((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}

\displaystyle=\left\{R\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}[R,1_{-}]=0\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{(\varPhi,0,Q,0,0,t)\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\begin{array}[]{l}\varPhi\in(\mathfrak{e}_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C},Q\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C},t\in C\end{array}\right\}.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\dim_{C}(((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}})=21+14+1=36.

Proof..

(1) The Lie algebra of the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is
\mbox{\rm{Der}}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})\cong(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
.

(2) Using the result of
(1)
above, by the straightforward computation, we can easily obtain the required results.
∎

We will prove the following proposition needed in the proof of connectedness.

Proposition 6.6
.

The group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
is a semi-direct product of groups
\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))
and
(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
:

\displaystyle((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))\rtimes(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}.

In particular, the group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
is connected.

Proof..

Let
((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}=\{(0,0,L,0,0,v)\,|\,L\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C},v\in C\}
be a Lie subalgebra of the Lie algebra

((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
(Lemma
6.5
(2)). Since it follows from
[L_{-},v_{-}]=0
that
\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-})
commutes with
\mbox{\rm{ad}}(v_{-})
, we have
\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-}+v_{-}))=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-}))\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(v_{-}))
. Hence the group
\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))
is the connected subgroup of the group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1-}
.

Now, let
\alpha\in((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1-}
and set
\alpha\tilde{1}:=(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t),\alpha 1^{-}:=(\varPhi_{1},P_{1},Q_{1},r_{1},s_{1},t_{1})
. Then, it follows from

\displaystyle[\alpha\tilde{1},1_{-}]

\displaystyle=\alpha[\tilde{1},1_{-}]=-2\alpha 1_{-}=-21_{-}=(0,0,0,0,0,-2),

\displaystyle[\alpha\tilde{1},1_{-}]

\displaystyle=[(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t),(0,0,0,0,0,1)]=(0,0,-P,s,0,-2r)

that
P=0,r=1,s=0
. Similarly, from
[\alpha 1^{-},1_{-}]=\alpha[1^{-},1_{-}]=\alpha\tilde{1}
, we have
\varPhi=0,P_{1}=-Q,s_{1}=r,s=0,t=-2r_{1}
. Hence we have

\displaystyle\alpha\tilde{1}=(0,0,Q,1,0,t),\quad\alpha 1^{-}=(\varPhi_{1},-Q,Q_{1},-\displaystyle\frac{t}{2},1,t_{1}).

Moreover, from
[\alpha\tilde{1},\alpha 1^{-}]=\alpha[\tilde{1},1^{-}]=2\alpha 1^{-}
, we obtain the following

\varPhi_{1}=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}Q\times Q,\;Q_{1}=-\displaystyle\frac{t}{2}Q-\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\varPhi_{1}Q,\;t_{1}=-\displaystyle\frac{t^{2}}{4}-\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}\{Q,Q_{1}\},

that is,

\displaystyle\alpha 1^{-}=(\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}Q\times Q,-Q,-\displaystyle\frac{t}{2}Q-\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}(Q\times Q)Q,-\displaystyle\frac{t}{2},1,-\displaystyle\frac{t^{2}}{4}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{96}\{Q,(Q\times Q)Q).

On the other hand, set
\delta:=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}((v/2)_{-}))\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-}))\in\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))
, then we have

\displaystyle\delta 1^{-}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\Big(\displaystyle\frac{v}{2}\Big)_{-})\Big)\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-}))1^{-}

\displaystyle=(\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}L\times L,-L,-\displaystyle\frac{t}{2}L-\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}(L\times L)L,-\displaystyle\frac{v}{2},1,-\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}}{4}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{96}\{L,(L\times L)L\}).

Hence we see

\displaystyle(\delta^{-1}\alpha)1^{-}=1^{-}.

In addition, we can confirm

\displaystyle(\delta^{-1}\alpha)\tilde{1}=\tilde{1},\,\,(\delta^{-1}\alpha)1_{-}=1_{-}.

Indeed, it follows from

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{1}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\Big(\displaystyle\frac{v}{2}\Big)_{-})\Big)\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-}))\tilde{1}=\exp\Big(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\Big(\displaystyle\frac{v}{2}\Big)_{-})\Big)(0,0,L,1,0,0)=(0,0,L,1,0,v),

\displaystyle\delta 1_{-}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\Big(\displaystyle\frac{v}{2}\Big)_{-})\Big)\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-}))1_{-}=\exp\Big(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\Big(\displaystyle\frac{v}{2}\Big)_{-})\Big)1_{-}=1_{-}

that
(\delta^{-1}\alpha)\tilde{1}=\delta^{-1}(0,0,P,1,0,t)=\tilde{1},(\delta^{-1}\alpha)1_{-}=\delta^{-1}1_{-}=1_{-}
.

Hence,
\delta^{-1}\alpha\in((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{\tilde{1},1^{-},1_{-}}=(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
follows from Proposition
6.4
,
so that we obtain

((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}.

Furthermore, for
\beta\in(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, it is easy to verify that

\beta(\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-})))\beta^{-1}=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\beta L_{-})),\quad\beta((\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(v_{-})))\beta^{-1}=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(v_{-})).

Indeed, for
(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t)\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, by doing simple computation, we have the following

\displaystyle\beta\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-})\beta^{-1}(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t)

\displaystyle=\beta[L_{-},\beta^{-1}(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t)]

\displaystyle=[\beta L_{-},\beta\beta^{-1}(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t)]\,(\beta\in(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\subset(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})

\displaystyle=[\beta L_{-},(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t)]

\displaystyle=\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\beta L_{-})(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t),

that is,
\beta\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-})\beta^{-1}=\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\beta L_{-})
.
Hence we obtain the following

\displaystyle\beta(\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-})))\beta^{-1}

\displaystyle=\beta\,\Bigl(\displaystyle{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{1}{n!}\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-})^{n}}\Bigr)\,\beta^{-1}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{1}{n!}(\beta\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-})\beta^{-1})^{n}}\,\,\,(\,\beta\mbox{\rm{ad}}(L_{-})\beta^{-1}=\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\beta L_{-}))

\displaystyle=\displaystyle{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{1}{n!}(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\beta L_{-}))^{n}}

\displaystyle=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(\beta L_{-})).

As for another case, by the argument similar to above, we have
\beta((\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(v_{-})))\beta^{-1}=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(v_{-})
.

Hence this shows that
\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-})\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(C_{-}))
is a normal

subgroup of the group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
.
In addition, we have a split exact sequence

1\to\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))\overset{j}{\longrightarrow}((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}\overset{\overset{p}{\scalebox{1.0}{$\longrightarrow$}}}{\underset{s}{\longleftarrow}}(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\to 1.

Indeed, as that in the case where
(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, we can show this, however we rewrite the proof. First, we define a mapping
j
by
j(\delta)=\delta
. Then it is clear that
j
is a injective homomorphism. Subsequently, we define a mapping
p
by
p(\alpha):=p(\delta\beta)=\beta,\delta\in\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-})),\beta\in(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
. Then it follows that

\displaystyle p(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle=p((\delta_{1}\beta_{1})(\delta_{2}\beta_{2}))=p(\delta_{1}(\beta_{1}\delta_{2}{\beta_{1}}^{-1})\beta_{1}\beta_{2})\,\,(\beta_{1}\delta_{2}{\beta_{1}}^{-1}=:{\delta_{2}}^{\prime}\in\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))

\displaystyle=p((\delta_{1}{\delta_{2}}^{\prime})(\beta_{1}\beta_{2}))=\beta_{1}\beta_{2}

\displaystyle=p(\alpha_{1})p(\alpha_{2}),

so that
p
is a homomorphism. Moreover, let
\beta\in(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, then there exists
\alpha\in((E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{\underset{\dot{}}{1}}
such that
\alpha=\delta\beta
for
\delta\in\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))
. This implies that
p
is surjective. Finally, we define a mapping
s
by
s(\beta):=s(\delta^{-1}\alpha)=\alpha
. Then, as in the mapping
p
, we easily see that
s
is a homomorphism and
ps=1
. With above, the short sequence is a split exact sequence.

Therefore the group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
is a semi-direct product of
\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))
and
(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
:

\displaystyle((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}=\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))\rtimes(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}.

Finally, the connectedness of the group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
follows from the connectedness of the groups

\exp(\mbox{\rm{ad}}(((\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{-}\oplus C_{-}))
and
(E_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
(Theorem
5.8
).
∎

Before proving the connectedness, we will make some preparations.

For
R\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, we define a
C
-linear mapping
R\times R:(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\to(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
by

(R\times R)R_{1}=[R,[R,R_{1}]\,]+\displaystyle\frac{1}{30}B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R,R_{1})R,\,\,R_{1}\in{\mathfrak{e}_{8}}^{C},

where
B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
is the Killing form of
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
.
Using this mapping, we define a space
\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
by

\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}=\{R\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\,|\,R\times R=0,R\not=0\}.

Lemma 6.7
.

For
R=(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t)\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
,

R\not=0
belongs to
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
if and only if
R
satisfies the following conditions

(1)

2s\varPhi-P\times P=0

(2)

2t\varPhi+Q\times Q=0

(3)

2r\varPhi+P\times Q=0

(4)

\varPhi P-3rP-3sQ=0

(5)

\varPhi Q+3rQ-3tP=0

(6)

\{P,Q\}-16(st+r^{2})=0

(7)

2(\varPhi P\times Q_{1}+2P\times\varPhi Q_{1}-rP\times Q_{1}-sQ\times Q_{1})-\{P,Q_{1}\}\varPhi=0

(8)

2(\varPhi Q\times P_{1}+2Q\times\varPhi P_{1}+rQ\times P_{1}-tP\times P_{1})\!-\{Q,P_{1}\}\varPhi=0

(9)

8((P\times Q_{1})Q-stQ_{1}-r^{2}Q_{1}-\varPhi^{2}Q_{1}+2r\varPhi Q_{1})+5\{P,Q_{1}\}Q-2\{Q,Q_{1}\}P=0

(10)

8((Q\times P_{1})P+stP_{1}+r^{2}P_{1}+\varPhi^{2}P_{1}+2r\varPhi P_{1})\,+\,5\{Q,P_{1}\}P-2\{P,Q_{1}\}Q=0

(11)

18(\mbox{\rm{ad}}\,\varPhi)^{2}\varPhi_{1}+Q\times\varPhi_{1}P-P\times\varPhi_{1}Q)+B_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(\varPhi,\varPhi_{1})\varPhi=0

(12)

18(\varPhi_{1}\varPhi P-2\varPhi\varPhi_{1}P-r\varPhi_{1}P-s\varPhi_{1}Q)+B_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(\varPhi,\varPhi_{1})P=0

(13)

18(\varPhi_{1}\varPhi Q-2\varPhi\varPhi_{1}Q+r\varPhi_{1}Q-t\varPhi_{1}P)+B_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(\varPhi,\varPhi_{1})Q=0,

for all
\varPhi_{1}\in(\mathfrak{e}_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C},P_{1},Q_{1}\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, where
B_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
is the Killing form of the Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{e}_{7,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
.

Proof..

For
R=(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t)\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, by doing simple computation of
(R\,\times\,R)R_{1}=0
for all
R_{1}=(\varPhi_{1},P_{1},Q_{1},r_{1},

s_{1},t_{1})\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, we have the required relational formulas above.
∎

Proposition 6.8
.

The group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{0}
acts on
\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
transitively.

Proof..

Since
\alpha\in(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
leaves the Killing form
B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
invariant
:B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(\alpha R,

\alpha R^{\prime})=B_{8}(R,R^{\prime}),R,R^{\prime}\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
acts on
\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
.
Indeed, let
R\in\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
. Then, for all
R_{1}\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, it follows that

\displaystyle(\alpha R\times\alpha R)R_{1}

\displaystyle=[\alpha R,[\alpha R,\alpha R_{1}]\,]+\displaystyle\frac{1}{30}B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(\alpha R,R_{1})\alpha R

\displaystyle=\alpha[\,[R,[R,\alpha^{-1}R_{1}]\,]+\displaystyle\frac{1}{30}B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R,\alpha^{-1}R_{1})\alpha R

\displaystyle=\alpha((R\times R)\alpha^{-1}R_{1}

\displaystyle=0.

Hence the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
acts on
\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
, that is,
\alpha R\in(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
.

We will show that this action is transitive. First, for all
R_{1}\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, it follows from

\displaystyle(1_{-}\times 1_{-})R_{1}

\displaystyle=[1_{-},[1_{-},(\varPhi_{1},P_{1},Q_{1},r_{1},s_{1},t_{1})]\,]+\displaystyle\frac{1}{30}B_{8}(1_{-},R_{1})1_{-}

\displaystyle=[1_{-},(0,0,P_{1},-s_{1},0,2r_{1})]+2s_{1}1_{-}

\displaystyle=(0,0,0,0,-2s_{1})+2s_{1}1_{-}

\displaystyle=0

that
1_{-}\in\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
. Then, any element
R\in\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
can be transformed to
1_{-}\in\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
by some
\alpha\in((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{0}
. This will be proved the following.

Case (i) where
R=(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t),t\not=0
.

From Lemma
6.7
(2),(5) and (6), we see

\varPhi=-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2t}Q\times Q,\;P=\displaystyle\frac{r}{t}Q-\displaystyle\frac{1}{6t^{2}}(Q\times Q)Q,\;s=-\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{t}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{96t^{3}}\{Q,(Q\times Q)Q\}.

Now, let
\varTheta:=\mbox{\rm{ad}}(0,P_{1},0,r_{1},s_{1},0)\in\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}),r_{1}\not=0
(Lemma
6.7
(1)), so we compute
\varTheta^{n}1_{-}
:

\displaystyle\quad\varTheta^{n}1_{-}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}((-2)^{n-1}+(-1)^{n}){r_{1}}^{n-2}P_{1}\times P_{1}\vskip 1.42262pt\\
\Big((-2)^{n-1}-\displaystyle\frac{1+(-1)^{n-1}}{2}\Big){r_{1}}^{n-2}s_{1}P_{1}+\Big(\displaystyle\frac{1-(-2)^{n}}{6}+\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{n}}{2}\Big){r_{1}}^{n-3}(P_{1}\times P_{1})P_{1}\vskip 1.42262pt\\
((-2)^{n}+(-1)^{n-1}){r_{1}}^{n-1}P_{1}\vskip 1.42262pt\\
(-2)^{n-1}{r_{1}}^{n-1}s_{1}\vskip 1.42262pt\\
-((-2)^{n-2}+2^{n-2}){r_{1}}^{n-2}{s_{1}}^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{2^{n-2}+(-2)^{n-2}-(-1)^{n-1}-1}{24}{r_{1}}^{n-4}\{P_{1},(P_{1}\times P_{1})P_{1}\}\vskip 1.42262pt\\
(-2)^{n}{r_{1}}^{n}\end{pmatrix}.

Then, by doing straightforward computation, we have

\displaystyle\quad(\exp\varTheta)1_{-}=\Big(\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{1}{n!}\varTheta^{n}\Big)1_{-}\vskip 1.42262pt

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2{r_{1}}^{2}}(e^{-2r_{1}}-2e^{-r_{1}}+1)P_{1}\times P_{1}\vskip 1.42262pt\\
\displaystyle\frac{s_{1}}{2{r_{1}}^{2}}(-e^{-2r_{1}}-e^{r_{1}}+e^{-r_{1}}+1)P_{1}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{6{r_{1}}^{3}}(-e^{-2r_{1}}+e^{r_{1}}+3e^{-r_{1}}-3)(P_{1}\times P_{1})P_{1}\vskip 1.42262pt\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{1}}(e^{-2r_{1}}-e^{-r_{1}})P_{1}\vskip 1.42262pt\\
\displaystyle\frac{s_{1}}{2r_{1}}(1-e^{-2r_{1}})\vskip 1.42262pt\\
-\displaystyle\frac{{s_{1}}^{2}}{4{r_{1}}^{2}}(e^{-2r_{1}}+e^{2r_{1}}-2)+\displaystyle\frac{1}{96{r_{1}}^{4}}(e^{2r_{1}}+e^{-2r_{1}}-4e^{r_{1}}-4e^{-r_{1}}+6)\{P_{1},(P_{1}\times P_{1})P_{1}\}\vskip 1.42262pt\\
e^{-2r_{1}}\end{pmatrix}.

Here, in the case where
t\not=1
, for a given
Q\in(\mathfrak{J}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C},r,t\in C
in Case (i) above,
we can choose
P_{1}\in(\mathfrak{J}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C},r_{1},s_{1}\in C
satisfying the conditions

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{1}}(e^{-2r_{1}}-e^{-r_{1}})P_{1}=Q,\;\;\displaystyle\frac{s_{1}}{2r_{1}}(1-e^{-2r_{1}})=r,\;\;e^{-2r_{1}}=t.

Indeed,

and some one value of

for

, respectively. Then because of

, we can get

\displaystyle P_{1}=\displaystyle\frac{(\sqrt{t}+t)\log t}{2(t-t^{2})}Q,\;\;r_{1}=-\displaystyle\frac{\log t}{2},\;\;s_{1}=\displaystyle\frac{\log t}{t-1}r

as
P_{1},r_{1},s_{1}
satisfying the conditions above.
Hence, by using
P_{1},r_{1},s_{1}
obtained above, we obtain

\displaystyle(\exp\varTheta)1_{-}=\begin{pmatrix}-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2t}Q\times Q\vskip 2.84526pt\\

Q\vskip 0.0pt\\
r\vskip 0.0pt\\
-\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{t}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{96t^{3}}\{Q,(Q\times Q)Q\}\vskip 0.0pt\\
t\end{pmatrix}=R.

In the case where

. Then, for a given
R=(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,t),t\not=0
,
R
is of the form

\displaystyle\varPhi=-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}Q\times Q,\;\;P=rQ-\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}(Q\times Q)Q,\;\;s=-r^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{96}\{Q,(Q\times Q)Q\},

so let
\varTheta:=\mbox{\rm{ad}}(0,P_{1},0,0,s_{1},0)\in\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})

\displaystyle(\exp\varTheta)1_{-}=\Big(\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{1}{n!}\varTheta^{n}\Big)1_{-}=\begin{pmatrix}-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}P_{1}\times P_{1}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
-s_{1}P_{1}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}(P_{1}\times P_{1})P_{1}\vskip 0.0pt\\
-P_{1}\vskip 0.0pt\\
s_{1}\vskip 0.0pt\\
-{s_{1}}^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{96}\{P_{1},(P_{1}\times P_{1})P_{1}\}\vskip 0.0pt\\
1\end{pmatrix}.

Here, as in the case where

, we choose

satisfying

. Hence, by using these

, we obtain

\displaystyle(\exp\varTheta)1_{-}=\begin{pmatrix}-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}Q\times Q\vskip 2.84526pt\\
rQ-\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}(Q\times Q)Q\vskip 0.0pt\\
Q\vskip 0.0pt\\

-r^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{96}\{Q,(Q\times Q)Q\}\vskip 0.0pt\\

Thus

by

.

Case (ii) where
R=(\varPhi,P,Q,r,s,0),s\not=0
.

Let
\varTheta:=\mbox{\rm{ad}}(0,0,0,0,{\pi}/{2},-{\pi}/{2}))\in(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
. Then we have

(\exp\varTheta)R=(\varPhi,Q,-P,-r,0,-s),\;\;\;-s\not=0.

Hence this case can be reduced to Case (i).

Case (iii) where
R=(\varPhi,P,Q,r,0,0),r\not=0
.

From Lemma
6.7
(2),(5) and (6), we have

Q\times Q=0,\;\;\varPhi Q=-3rQ,\;\;\{P,Q\}=16r^{2}.

Then, let
\mbox{\rm{ad}}(0,Q,0,0,0,0)\in\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})
(Lemma

(1)), we have

(\exp\mbox{\rm{ad}}(0,Q,0,0,0,0))R=(\varPhi,P+2rQ,Q,r,-4r^{2},0),\;\;-4r^{2}\not=0.

Hence this case can be reduced to Case (ii).

Case (iv) where
R=(\varPhi,P,Q,0,0,0),Q\not=0
.

We can choose
P_{1}\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
such that
\{P_{1},Q\}\not=0
. Indeed, for all
P_{1}\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, suppose
\{P_{1},Q\}=0
. Then we have
Q=0
. This is contradiction. Hence there exists
P_{1}\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
such that
\{P_{1},Q\}\not=0
.

Now, let
\varTheta:=\mbox{\rm{ad}}(0,P_{1},0,0,0,0)\in\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})
(Lemma
6.5
(1)), we have

\displaystyle(\exp\varTheta)R=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varPhi+P_{1}\times Q\\
\,P-\varPhi P_{1}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(P_{1}\times Q)P_{1}\\
Q\\
-\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\{P_{1},Q\}\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\{P_{1},P\}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\{P_{1},-\varPhi P_{1}\}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{24}\{P_{1},(P_{1}\times Q)P_{1}\}\\
0\end{array}\right),\;\;-\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\{P_{1},Q\}\not=0.

Hence this case can be reduced to Case (iii).

Case (v) where
R=(\varPhi,P,0,0,0,0),P\not=0
.

As in Case (iv), we choose
Q_{1}\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{C}$}})^{C}
such that
\{P,Q_{1}\}\not=0
. Then, let
\varTheta:=\mbox{\rm{ad}}(0,0,Q_{1},0,0,0)\in\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})
(Lemma
6.5
(1)), we have

\displaystyle(\exp\varTheta)R=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varPhi-P\times Q_{1}\\
P\\
-\varPhi Q_{1}-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(P\times Q_{1})Q_{1}\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\{P,Q_{1}\}\\
0\\
-\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\{Q_{1},-\varPhi Q_{1}\}-\displaystyle\frac{1}{24}\{Q_{1},-(P\times Q_{1})Q_{1}\}\end{array}\right),\;\;\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\{P,Q_{1}\}\not=0.

Hence this case can be reduced to Case (iii).

Case (vi) where
R=(\varPhi,0,0,0,0,0),\varPhi\not=0.

From Lemma
6.7
(9) or (10), we have
\varPhi^{2}=0
.
We choose
P_{1}\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{C}$}})^{C}
such that
\varPhi P_{1}\not=0
. Indeed, for all
P_{1}\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
, suppose
\varPhi P_{1}=0
. Then we have
\varPhi=0
. This is contradiction. Hence there exists
P_{1}\in(\mathfrak{P}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
such that
\varPhi P_{1}\not=0
.
Then, let
\varTheta:=\mbox{\rm{ad}}(0,P_{1},0,0,0,0)\in\mbox{\rm{ad}}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})
(Lemma
6.5
(1)), we have

(\exp\mbox{\rm{ad}}(0,P_{1},0,0,0,0))R=\Big(\varPhi,-\varPhi P_{1},0,0,\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\{\varPhi P_{1},P_{1}\},0\Big),\;\;-\varPhi P_{1}\not=0.

Hence this case is also reduced to Case (v).

With above, the proof of this proposition is completed.
∎

Now, we will prove the theorem as the aim of this section.

Theorem 6.9
.

The homogeneous space
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}/((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
is diffeomorphic to the space
\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
:

(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}/((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}\simeq\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
.

In particular, the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is connected.

Proof..

Since the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
acts on the space
\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
transitively (Proposition
6.8
), the former half of this theorem is proved.

The latter half can be shown as follows. Since the group
((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{1_{-}}
and the space
\mathfrak{W}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}=((E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})_{0}1_{-}
are connected (Propositions
6.6
,
6.8
),
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is also connected.
∎

We will determine the structure of the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
.

Theorem 6.10
.

The group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is isomorphic to the group
{F_{4}}^{C}
:

(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\cong{F_{4}}^{C}
.

Proof..

Since the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is connected (Theorem
6.9
) and the type of its associated Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is
F_{4}
(Theorem
6.3
) and the center
z({F_{4}}^{C})
of the group
{F_{4}}^{C}
is trivial.

Therefore the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
has be isomorphic to the group
{F_{4}}^{C}
:

\displaystyle(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\cong{F_{4}}^{C}.

∎

We consider a real form
E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
of
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
:

\displaystyle E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}=\left\{\alpha\in(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\langle\alpha R_{1},\alpha R_{2}\rangle=\langle R_{1},R_{2}\rangle\right\},

where the Hermite inner product
\langle R_{1},R_{2}\rangle
is defined by
(-1/15)B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R_{1},\tau\lambda_{\omega}R_{2})
:
\langle R_{1},R_{2}\rangle=(-1/15)\allowbreak B_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}(R_{1},\tau\lambda_{\omega}R_{2})
. Then this group is compact Lie group as the closed subgroup of the unitary group
U(52)=\left\{\alpha\in\mbox{\rm{Iso}}_{C}((\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C})\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\langle\alpha R_{1},\alpha R_{2}\rangle=\langle R_{1},R_{2}\rangle\right\}
.

Now, we will determine the structure of the group
E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
.

Theorem 6.11
.

The group
E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
is isomorphic to the group
F_{4}
:

E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}\cong F_{4}
.

Proof..

Since the complex Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{e}_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
of the group
(E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}})^{C}
is the type of
F_{4}
(Theorem
6.3
), its real form is also the type
F_{4}
. Hence the real form of the complex Lie group
E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
is isomorphic to one of the following groups

\displaystyle F_{4},\,\,F_{4(4)},\,\,F_{4(-20)}.

However,
E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
is compact.

Therefore
E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}
have to be isomorphic to the compact Lie group
F_{4}
:

\displaystyle E_{8,\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}\cong F_{4}.

∎
